response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three sums of module bilinearintrp — r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum, each now carrying all three products of that sum after the w term is folded into the two product slots
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • blk_r (sum of r): clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dr} with dr = ru - rw (7-bit wrap), bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,er} with er = rv - rw, chainin=37'b0, result→new wire sum_r[36:0], chainout unconnected.
  • blk_g: same, ay={12'b0,dg} with dg = gu - gw, by={12'b0,eg} with eg = gv - gw, result→sum_g[36:0].
  • blk_b: same, ay={12'b0,db} with db = bu - bw, by={12'b0,eb} with eb = bv - bw, result→sum_b[36:0].
  • u, v are the combinational 8-bit selectuv mux outputs (always block, lines 2818-2858), fed unregistered; the block's input registers replace ul, vl. ru,rv,rw,gu,gv,gw,bu,bv,bw are module inputs (driven at line 2012 from shadedata, which is tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input — lines 628-676); the 7-bit differences are combinational on them and the block's input registers replace rul, rvl, rwl, gul, ….
  • Outside the block, per colour, two added 7-bit delay registers (rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2) and a 7-bit subtract: assign r = sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2; and likewise for g, b.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The kept pass put six of the nine products into three blocks and left wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl as soft multipliers, because a third product per sum would need a chained second block and that costs a cycle. This pass removes those three soft multipliers without a fourth block and without a latency change, by folding the w term algebraically into the two product slots the block already has. The measured critical path of the kept pass ends at a block input register (mux → DSP input), and the block inputs here come from primary-input pads for the colour operands, so this change does not lengthen any timed path; it only deletes logic (3 soft 8×7 multipliers, the 255-u-v adder, and 74 flip-flops, against 6 small 7-bit subtracters and 42 flip-flops added), and it also drops two fanout branches (wl adder, i3* multipliers) off the critical mux outputs u/v.

The algebra (exact, not approximate). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits wide (lines 2786-2791), so only the sum mod 2^7 is observable. For colour r, with wl = (255-u-v) mod 256:

r ≡ u*ru + v*rv + wl*rw (mod 128)

The mod-256 wrap of wl contributes multiples of 256*rw ≡ 0 (mod 128), so wl*rw ≡ (255-u-v)*rw, and 255-u-v = 256-(u+v+1) ≡ -(u+v+1) (mod 128). Hence

r ≡ u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128)

Let dr = (ru-rw) mod 128 and er = (rv-rw) mod 128 (plain 7-bit two's-complement wrap-around subtraction of two 7-bit values). Since u*(dr-(ru-rw)) is u times a multiple of 128, u*dr ≡ u*(ru-rw) (mod 128), and likewise for v. So

r ≡ (u*dr + v*er) - rw (mod 128)

which is exactly result[6:0] - rw_delayed. The block reads its operands unsigned: u,v ≤ 255, dr,er ≤ 127, so each product ≤ 32385 and the sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^37, nothing wraps in the block, and the unsigned reading matches. Same for g and b.

Register stages. Original, per converted region: 2 stages from the operands (u/v from the mux, and the colour inputs) to the output — operand registers ul,vl,rul,rvl,rwl at edge k, product registers i1*,i2*,i3* at edge k+1, then a combinational add into the 7-bit output. After the rewrite: still 2 stages — the block's input registers at edge k, the block's result register at edge k+1, then one combinational 7-bit subtract. The folded w term keeps the same 2 stages through rw_d1 (edge k) and rw_d2 (edge k+1), so it is aligned with result and r/g/b change on exactly the same cycles as before. No chaining is used, so no block sees a 3-stage path.

The edit, concretely (module bilinearintrp, lines 2762-2887):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1r,i2r,i3r, i1g,i2g,i3g, i1b,i2b,i3b, rul,rvl,rwl, gul,gvl,gwl, bul,bvl,bwl (lines 2796-2816). Keep u, v (lines 2794-2795, the combinational mux outputs). Leave the mux always block (2818-2858) untouched.

  2. Add:

           wire[6:0] dr = ru - rw;  wire[6:0] er = rv - rw;
       wire[6:0] dg = gu - gw;  wire[6:0] eg = gv - gw;
       wire[6:0] db = bu - bw;  wire[6:0] eb = bv - bw;
       wire[36:0] sum_r, sum_g, sum_b;
       reg[6:0] rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2;

int_sop_2 blk_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dr}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,er}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_r), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_g (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dg}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eg}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_g), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_b (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,db}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eb}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_b), .chainout());

3. Replace the whole sequential always block (2860-2883) with:

always @(posedge clk)
begin
rw_d1 <= rw ; rw_d2 <= rw_d1 ;
gw_d1 <= gw ; gw_d2 <= gw_d1 ;
bw_d1 <= bw ; bw_d2 <= bw_d1 ;
end

4. Replace lines 2884-2886 with:

assign r = (sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2) ;
assign g = (sum_g[6:0] - gw_d2) ;
assign b = (sum_b[6:0] - bw_d2) ;


**Visibility.** Every signal removed (`ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl, i1*,i2*,i3*`) is a local `reg` of `bilinearintrp`; the module's only outputs are `r`, `g`, `b` (2786-2791). I checked the whole module body, 2762-2887: `ul`/`vl` are read only by the converted products, `wl` only by the three `i3*` products, the `*l` operand registers only by their own product. The `ul`/`vl` at lines 2752-2756 belong to the preceding module (which ends at 2761), not to this one. Nothing reads `chainout`; it is left unconnected on all three blocks.

**Reset and power-up.** `bilinearintrp` has no reset port and no register in it is ever cleared, so `reset` is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks; the testbench's reset policy is "none" (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so there is no reset event for which behaviour could diverge. The three block result registers and the six added delay registers are X at power-up and all load every edge, so every output is defined from the second clock edge — well inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in, which is excluded from the comparison. Nothing relies on an added register's initial value.

**Tie-offs.** `mode_sigs` = 12'b0 and `chainin` = 37'b0 (constant) on all three blocks; both product slots are used on every block, so no product-term port is tied to 0; every input port is connected by name.

**Candidates converted and left.** All nine candidates are converted; no soft multiplier remains in `bilinearintrp`. The six `u`/`v` products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) go into the blocks' product slots directly, each with its colour coefficient replaced by the difference against the `w` coefficient; the three `w` products (2876, 2879, 2882) are folded into those same two slots by the identity above, with only their constant part (`-rw`, `-gw`, `-bw` mod 128) left as a 7-bit subtract outside — a term with no matching block port, computed outside the block as the rules allow. No candidate is left for "not chosen this pass" and none is left for a correctness reason.

**Blocks added:** 3 (3 of the device's 132 DSP tiles, each in sop_2 mode taking its tile alone) — the same count as the kept pass, with three fewer soft multipliers and one fewer soft adder, which should also free DSP capacity for the remaining multipliers of lines 335-343 (the kept pass packed 7 tiles against the baseline's 6).

No ASSUMES: the rewrite is unconditional — it holds for every value of the inputs, including the testbench's stimulus, because the folding is an identity mod 2^7 and 2^7 is exactly the output width.

DESIGN_CHANGE: `r`, `g`, `b` are 7 bits while the products are computed at 15 bits; the intermediate `i1*/i2*/i3*` registers and the final adders could be declared at 7 bits directly, which is all the design observes. Separately, if one extra cycle of latency on `blr/blg/blb` (instance at line 2012) were acceptable, the three sums could instead be chained block-to-block, but that is not needed now that all nine products fit in three blocks.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.

BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • blk_r (sum of r): clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,ru}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,rv}, chainin=37'b0, result→new wire sum_r[36:0], chainout unconnected.
  • blk_g (sum of g): same, with ay={12'b0,gu}, by={12'b0,gv}, result→sum_g[36:0].
  • blk_b (sum of b): same, with ay={12'b0,bu}, by={12'b0,bv}, result→sum_b[36:0].
  • u and v are the combinational 8-bit selectuv mux outputs (always block, lines 2818-2858); ru/rv/gu/gv/bu/bv are the 7-bit module inputs. All are fed to the blocks unregistered — the block's own input registers take the place of the design registers ul, vl, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl.

PLAN:

What the region is. In bilinearintrp (lines 2762-2887) there are three independent sums, each of three products, each truncated to 7 bits:

  • stage 1 (edge k): ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'hFF-u-v, rul<=ru, rvl<=rv, rwl<=rw, and the same for g and b (lines 2862-2873);
  • stage 2 (edge k+1): i1r<=ul*rul, i2r<=vl*rvl, i3r<=wl*rwl, likewise for g,b (lines 2874-2882);
  • combinational: assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r); with r only 7 bits wide, so only the low 7 bits of each sum are observable (same for g, b).

So each output is 2 register stages behind the module inputs: values sampled at edge k appear on r/g/b right after edge k+1.

The rewrite. Per colour, one int_sop_2 takes the first two products of that colour's sum: result = u*ru + v*rv (and u*gu + v*gv, u*bu + v*bv). The block registers ax,ay,bx,by at edge k and result at edge k+1 — exactly the two stages the original spends on ul/rul then i1r/i2r, with the first adder of the chain moved from after the product registers to before the block's result register. Both products are ≤ 255*127 = 32385, their sum ≤ 64770, so nothing wraps at 37 bits and the block's unsigned reading matches the design's unsigned operands.

The third product of each sum (wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl) stays in soft logic exactly as written: keep the wl, rwl, gwl, bwl registers (lines 2862, 2867, 2870, 2873) and the i3r, i3g, i3b registers (lines 2876, 2879, 2882) unchanged.

Final adds, replacing lines 2884-2886:

assign r = sum_r[6:0] + i3r[6:0];
assign g = sum_g[6:0] + i3g[6:0];
assign b = sum_b[6:0] + i3b[6:0];

This is the original value mod 2^7, which is all a 7-bit output carries, so it is bit-identical to the original on every cycle.

Registers to delete (now dead; their function is inside the blocks): ul, vl, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl, i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b, and their assignments in the always block at 2860-2883. wl, rwl, gwl, bwl, i3r, i3g, i3b and their assignments stay. Nothing outside the module reads any of these (they are local regs; the only outputs are r, g, b), and inside the module ul/vl are read only by the products being converted — checked over the whole module body, lines 2762-2887.

Register stages, per converted region: before the rewrite, 2 stages from the operands (u/v and the colour input) to the sum that drives the output: operand register at edge k, product register at edge k+1, combinational add. After the rewrite, still 2 stages: block input register at edge k, block result register at edge k+1, combinational 7-bit add with i3*. The unconverted third product keeps its own 2 stages (wl/rwl at edge k, i3* at edge k+1), so all three terms of each sum stay aligned and the outputs change on exactly the same cycles as before.

Reset. bilinearintrp has no reset port and no register in it is ever cleared, so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks; there is no reset event in the design or the testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v, "reset policy: none") for which behaviour could differ. The block's registers are X at power-up and load at every edge, so result is defined from the second clock edge; the testbench compares only after its 8-cycle zero lead-in, well past that.

Tie-offs. mode_sigs = 12'b0 on all three blocks. chainin = 37'b0 on all three (no chaining — see below). Both product-term pairs are used on every block, so no product port is tied off. chainout is left unconnected and read by nothing.

Candidates left unconverted: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350 (wl*rwl), ...:2879$353 (wl*gwl), ...:2882$356 (wl*bwl). Correctness reason: each sum has three products and one block holds only two, so the third would need a second, chained block. Chaining costs a cycle — block 1's operands at edge k produce chainout during cycle k+1, block 2 registers that chainin at edge k+1 and shows it in result after edge k+2, i.e. 3 stages from the operands instead of the original 2, while the design offers no way to feed block 1 a cycle early. That would shift r/g/b by one cycle and break same-cycle equivalence, so these three products are kept in soft logic. No other candidate is left: the remaining six are all converted.

Blocks added: 3 (3 of the device's 132 DSP tiles).

DESIGN_CHANGE: If one extra cycle of latency on r, g, b were acceptable to the surrounding pipeline (the consumer of blr/blg/blb from the instance at line 2012), each colour's third product could go into a second block chained through chainout→chainin, putting all nine products into 6 blocks with no soft multiplier left. Separately, since r, g, b are only 7 bits, the design could state the products at 7-bit width directly; that is what the current arithmetic effectively computes.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three sums of module bilinearintrp — r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum, each now carrying all three products of that sum after the w term is folded into the two product slots
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • blk_r (sum of r): clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dr} with dr = ru - rw (7-bit wrap), bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,er} with er = rv - rw, chainin=37'b0, result→new wire sum_r[36:0], chainout unconnected.
  • blk_g: same, ay={12'b0,dg} with dg = gu - gw, by={12'b0,eg} with eg = gv - gw, result→sum_g[36:0].
  • blk_b: same, ay={12'b0,db} with db = bu - bw, by={12'b0,eb} with eb = bv - bw, result→sum_b[36:0].
  • u, v are the combinational 8-bit selectuv mux outputs (always block, lines 2818-2858), fed unregistered; the block's input registers replace ul, vl. ru,rv,rw,gu,gv,gw,bu,bv,bw are module inputs (driven at line 2012 from shadedata, which is tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input — lines 628-676); the 7-bit differences are combinational on them and the block's input registers replace rul, rvl, rwl, gul, ….
  • Outside the block, per colour, two added 7-bit delay registers (rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2) and a 7-bit subtract: assign r = sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2; and likewise for g, b.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The kept pass put six of the nine products into three blocks and left wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl as soft multipliers, because a third product per sum would need a chained second block and that costs a cycle. This pass removes those three soft multipliers without a fourth block and without a latency change, by folding the w term algebraically into the two product slots the block already has. The measured critical path of the kept pass ends at a block input register (mux → DSP input), and the block inputs here come from primary-input pads for the colour operands, so this change does not lengthen any timed path; it only deletes logic (3 soft 8×7 multipliers, the 255-u-v adder, and 74 flip-flops, against 6 small 7-bit subtracters and 42 flip-flops added), and it also drops two fanout branches (wl adder, i3* multipliers) off the critical mux outputs u/v.

The algebra (exact, not approximate). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits wide (lines 2786-2791), so only the sum mod 2^7 is observable. For colour r, with wl = (255-u-v) mod 256:

r ≡ u*ru + v*rv + wl*rw (mod 128)

The mod-256 wrap of wl contributes multiples of 256*rw ≡ 0 (mod 128), so wl*rw ≡ (255-u-v)*rw, and 255-u-v = 256-(u+v+1) ≡ -(u+v+1) (mod 128). Hence

r ≡ u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128)

Let dr = (ru-rw) mod 128 and er = (rv-rw) mod 128 (plain 7-bit two's-complement wrap-around subtraction of two 7-bit values). Since u*(dr-(ru-rw)) is u times a multiple of 128, u*dr ≡ u*(ru-rw) (mod 128), and likewise for v. So

r ≡ (u*dr + v*er) - rw (mod 128)

which is exactly result[6:0] - rw_delayed. The block reads its operands unsigned: u,v ≤ 255, dr,er ≤ 127, so each product ≤ 32385 and the sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^37, nothing wraps in the block, and the unsigned reading matches. Same for g and b.

Register stages. Original, per converted region: 2 stages from the operands (u/v from the mux, and the colour inputs) to the output — operand registers ul,vl,rul,rvl,rwl at edge k, product registers i1*,i2*,i3* at edge k+1, then a combinational add into the 7-bit output. After the rewrite: still 2 stages — the block's input registers at edge k, the block's result register at edge k+1, then one combinational 7-bit subtract. The folded w term keeps the same 2 stages through rw_d1 (edge k) and rw_d2 (edge k+1), so it is aligned with result and r/g/b change on exactly the same cycles as before. No chaining is used, so no block sees a 3-stage path.

The edit, concretely (module bilinearintrp, lines 2762-2887):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1r,i2r,i3r, i1g,i2g,i3g, i1b,i2b,i3b, rul,rvl,rwl, gul,gvl,gwl, bul,bvl,bwl (lines 2796-2816). Keep u, v (lines 2794-2795, the combinational mux outputs). Leave the mux always block (2818-2858) untouched.

  2. Add:

           wire[6:0] dr = ru - rw;  wire[6:0] er = rv - rw;
       wire[6:0] dg = gu - gw;  wire[6:0] eg = gv - gw;
       wire[6:0] db = bu - bw;  wire[6:0] eb = bv - bw;
       wire[36:0] sum_r, sum_g, sum_b;
       reg[6:0] rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2;

int_sop_2 blk_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dr}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,er}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_r), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_g (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dg}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eg}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_g), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_b (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,db}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eb}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_b), .chainout());

3. Replace the whole sequential always block (2860-2883) with:

always @(posedge clk)
begin
rw_d1 <= rw ; rw_d2 <= rw_d1 ;
gw_d1 <= gw ; gw_d2 <= gw_d1 ;
bw_d1 <= bw ; bw_d2 <= bw_d1 ;
end

4. Replace lines 2884-2886 with:

assign r = (sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2) ;
assign g = (sum_g[6:0] - gw_d2) ;
assign b = (sum_b[6:0] - bw_d2) ;


**Visibility.** Every signal removed (`ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl, i1*,i2*,i3*`) is a local `reg` of `bilinearintrp`; the module's only outputs are `r`, `g`, `b` (2786-2791). I checked the whole module body, 2762-2887: `ul`/`vl` are read only by the converted products, `wl` only by the three `i3*` products, the `*l` operand registers only by their own product. The `ul`/`vl` at lines 2752-2756 belong to the preceding module (which ends at 2761), not to this one. Nothing reads `chainout`; it is left unconnected on all three blocks.

**Reset and power-up.** `bilinearintrp` has no reset port and no register in it is ever cleared, so `reset` is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks; the testbench's reset policy is "none" (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so there is no reset event for which behaviour could diverge. The three block result registers and the six added delay registers are X at power-up and all load every edge, so every output is defined from the second clock edge — well inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in, which is excluded from the comparison. Nothing relies on an added register's initial value.

**Tie-offs.** `mode_sigs` = 12'b0 and `chainin` = 37'b0 (constant) on all three blocks; both product slots are used on every block, so no product-term port is tied to 0; every input port is connected by name.

**Candidates converted and left.** All nine candidates are converted; no soft multiplier remains in `bilinearintrp`. The six `u`/`v` products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) go into the blocks' product slots directly, each with its colour coefficient replaced by the difference against the `w` coefficient; the three `w` products (2876, 2879, 2882) are folded into those same two slots by the identity above, with only their constant part (`-rw`, `-gw`, `-bw` mod 128) left as a 7-bit subtract outside — a term with no matching block port, computed outside the block as the rules allow. No candidate is left for "not chosen this pass" and none is left for a correctness reason.

**Blocks added:** 3 (3 of the device's 132 DSP tiles, each in sop_2 mode taking its tile alone) — the same count as the kept pass, with three fewer soft multipliers and one fewer soft adder, which should also free DSP capacity for the remaining multipliers of lines 335-343 (the kept pass packed 7 tiles against the baseline's 6).

No ASSUMES: the rewrite is unconditional — it holds for every value of the inputs, including the testbench's stimulus, because the folding is an identity mod 2^7 and 2^7 is exactly the output width.

DESIGN_CHANGE: `r`, `g`, `b` are 7 bits while the products are computed at 15 bits; the intermediate `i1*/i2*/i3*` registers and the final adders could be declared at 7 bits directly, which is all the design observes. Separately, if one extra cycle of latency on `blr/blg/blb` (instance at line 2012) were acceptable, the three sums could instead be chained block-to-block, but that is not needed now that all nine products fit in three blocks.

## Reference simulation
After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of `bilinearintrp` on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

APPROVE

Verification performed against /work/raygentop.v, sim_reference/int_sop_2.v, sim_reference/tb_equiv_bilin.v:

Design claims

  • Module bilinearintrp spans 2762–2887; outputs are only r, g, b (2786–2791), all 7 bits — confirmed.
  • Declarations to delete are exactly 2796–2816 (ul, vl, wl, i1r/i2r/i3r, i1g/i2g/i3g, i1b/i2b/i3b, rul…bwl); u, v at 2794–2795 are the combinational mux outputs (2818–2858) and are kept — confirmed.
  • No reset port, and the sequential block at 2860–2883 has no clear of any register, so reset=1'b0 is right; the testbench declares reset policy none (tb_equiv_bilin.v:5) — confirmed.
  • ul/vl at 2743–2756 are in the preceding module, which ends at 2761 — confirmed; nothing outside reads any deleted signal.
  • shadedata = tm3_sram_data_in (676), driven into the instance at 2012; multipliers at 335–343 — confirmed.

Arithmetic

  • wl <= 8'hFF-u-v wraps mod 256; wl*rw ≡ (255-u-v)*rw (mod 128) since 256·rw ≡ 0 (mod 128), and 255 ≡ −1 (mod 128), giving r ≡ u*(ru−rw) + v*(rv−rw) − rw (mod 128). Replacing the coefficients with 7-bit wrapped dr, er is exact because u*128m ≡ 0 (mod 128).
  • Original assign r = i1r+i2r+i3r evaluates at 15 bits (wrap mod 2^15) then truncates to 7; 2^7 | 2^15, so the original is exactly the sum mod 128. Spot-checked numerically (u=200,v=100,ru=3,rv=5,rw=7 → 17 both ways; u=v=255,ru=100,rv=50,rw=127 → 105 both ways), including the default selectuv case (u=v=0 → −rw both ways).
  • Block operands ≤ 255 and ≤ 127, so u*dr+v*er ≤ 64770 — no 37-bit wrap, unsigned reading matches. No ASSUMES is needed; the identity is unconditional, so it also holds on the testbench's stimulus.

Timing

  • sum_r after edge k+1 reflects u,v,ru,rw sampled before edge k (block registers operands at k, result at k+1); rw_d2 after edge k+1 holds rw from before edge k. Aligned with the original's ul/rul → i1r/i3r → combinational add. Outputs change on the same cycles; 2 stages before and after, no chaining, so no 3-stage path.

Contract

  • All eight input ports connected by name; mode_sigs=12'b0; chainin=37'b0 constant; both product slots used so no product-term tie-off needed; chainout left unconnected and read by nothing; 3 blocks of 132 tiles.
  • Power-up: block result and the added delay registers are defined from the second edge, inside the 8-cycle lead-in excluded from comparison (cmp set after LEAD_IN ticks).

Required items — all nine candidate regions accounted for (all converted, the three w products folded into the same two slots with their constant part computed outside), block count stated (3), and register stages before/after stated per converted region.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356. The plan names their shared sum as 'the three sums of module bilinearintrp — r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum, each now carrying all three products of that sum after the w term is folded into the two product slots'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three sums of module bilinearintrp — r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum, each now carrying all three products of that sum after the w term is folded into the two product slots
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • blk_r (sum of r): clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dr} with dr = ru - rw (7-bit wrap), bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,er} with er = rv - rw, chainin=37'b0, result→new wire sum_r[36:0], chainout unconnected.
  • blk_g: same, ay={12'b0,dg} with dg = gu - gw, by={12'b0,eg} with eg = gv - gw, result→sum_g[36:0].
  • blk_b: same, ay={12'b0,db} with db = bu - bw, by={12'b0,eb} with eb = bv - bw, result→sum_b[36:0].
  • u, v are the combinational 8-bit selectuv mux outputs (always block, lines 2818-2858), fed unregistered; the block's input registers replace ul, vl. ru,rv,rw,gu,gv,gw,bu,bv,bw are module inputs (driven at line 2012 from shadedata, which is tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input — lines 628-676); the 7-bit differences are combinational on them and the block's input registers replace rul, rvl, rwl, gul, ….
  • Outside the block, per colour, two added 7-bit delay registers (rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2) and a 7-bit subtract: assign r = sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2; and likewise for g, b.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The kept pass put six of the nine products into three blocks and left wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl as soft multipliers, because a third product per sum would need a chained second block and that costs a cycle. This pass removes those three soft multipliers without a fourth block and without a latency change, by folding the w term algebraically into the two product slots the block already has. The measured critical path of the kept pass ends at a block input register (mux → DSP input), and the block inputs here come from primary-input pads for the colour operands, so this change does not lengthen any timed path; it only deletes logic (3 soft 8×7 multipliers, the 255-u-v adder, and 74 flip-flops, against 6 small 7-bit subtracters and 42 flip-flops added), and it also drops two fanout branches (wl adder, i3* multipliers) off the critical mux outputs u/v.

The algebra (exact, not approximate). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits wide (lines 2786-2791), so only the sum mod 2^7 is observable. For colour r, with wl = (255-u-v) mod 256:

r ≡ u*ru + v*rv + wl*rw (mod 128)

The mod-256 wrap of wl contributes multiples of 256*rw ≡ 0 (mod 128), so wl*rw ≡ (255-u-v)*rw, and 255-u-v = 256-(u+v+1) ≡ -(u+v+1) (mod 128). Hence

r ≡ u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128)

Let dr = (ru-rw) mod 128 and er = (rv-rw) mod 128 (plain 7-bit two's-complement wrap-around subtraction of two 7-bit values). Since u*(dr-(ru-rw)) is u times a multiple of 128, u*dr ≡ u*(ru-rw) (mod 128), and likewise for v. So

r ≡ (u*dr + v*er) - rw (mod 128)

which is exactly result[6:0] - rw_delayed. The block reads its operands unsigned: u,v ≤ 255, dr,er ≤ 127, so each product ≤ 32385 and the sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^37, nothing wraps in the block, and the unsigned reading matches. Same for g and b.

Register stages. Original, per converted region: 2 stages from the operands (u/v from the mux, and the colour inputs) to the output — operand registers ul,vl,rul,rvl,rwl at edge k, product registers i1*,i2*,i3* at edge k+1, then a combinational add into the 7-bit output. After the rewrite: still 2 stages — the block's input registers at edge k, the block's result register at edge k+1, then one combinational 7-bit subtract. The folded w term keeps the same 2 stages through rw_d1 (edge k) and rw_d2 (edge k+1), so it is aligned with result and r/g/b change on exactly the same cycles as before. No chaining is used, so no block sees a 3-stage path.

The edit, concretely (module bilinearintrp, lines 2762-2887):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1r,i2r,i3r, i1g,i2g,i3g, i1b,i2b,i3b, rul,rvl,rwl, gul,gvl,gwl, bul,bvl,bwl (lines 2796-2816). Keep u, v (lines 2794-2795, the combinational mux outputs). Leave the mux always block (2818-2858) untouched.

  2. Add:

           wire[6:0] dr = ru - rw;  wire[6:0] er = rv - rw;
       wire[6:0] dg = gu - gw;  wire[6:0] eg = gv - gw;
       wire[6:0] db = bu - bw;  wire[6:0] eb = bv - bw;
       wire[36:0] sum_r, sum_g, sum_b;
       reg[6:0] rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2;

int_sop_2 blk_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dr}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,er}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_r), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_g (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dg}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eg}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_g), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_b (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,db}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eb}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_b), .chainout());

3. Replace the whole sequential always block (2860-2883) with:

always @(posedge clk)
begin
rw_d1 <= rw ; rw_d2 <= rw_d1 ;
gw_d1 <= gw ; gw_d2 <= gw_d1 ;
bw_d1 <= bw ; bw_d2 <= bw_d1 ;
end

4. Replace lines 2884-2886 with:

assign r = (sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2) ;
assign g = (sum_g[6:0] - gw_d2) ;
assign b = (sum_b[6:0] - bw_d2) ;


**Visibility.** Every signal removed (`ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl, i1*,i2*,i3*`) is a local `reg` of `bilinearintrp`; the module's only outputs are `r`, `g`, `b` (2786-2791). I checked the whole module body, 2762-2887: `ul`/`vl` are read only by the converted products, `wl` only by the three `i3*` products, the `*l` operand registers only by their own product. The `ul`/`vl` at lines 2752-2756 belong to the preceding module (which ends at 2761), not to this one. Nothing reads `chainout`; it is left unconnected on all three blocks.

**Reset and power-up.** `bilinearintrp` has no reset port and no register in it is ever cleared, so `reset` is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks; the testbench's reset policy is "none" (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so there is no reset event for which behaviour could diverge. The three block result registers and the six added delay registers are X at power-up and all load every edge, so every output is defined from the second clock edge — well inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in, which is excluded from the comparison. Nothing relies on an added register's initial value.

**Tie-offs.** `mode_sigs` = 12'b0 and `chainin` = 37'b0 (constant) on all three blocks; both product slots are used on every block, so no product-term port is tied to 0; every input port is connected by name.

**Candidates converted and left.** All nine candidates are converted; no soft multiplier remains in `bilinearintrp`. The six `u`/`v` products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) go into the blocks' product slots directly, each with its colour coefficient replaced by the difference against the `w` coefficient; the three `w` products (2876, 2879, 2882) are folded into those same two slots by the identity above, with only their constant part (`-rw`, `-gw`, `-bw` mod 128) left as a 7-bit subtract outside — a term with no matching block port, computed outside the block as the rules allow. No candidate is left for "not chosen this pass" and none is left for a correctness reason.

**Blocks added:** 3 (3 of the device's 132 DSP tiles, each in sop_2 mode taking its tile alone) — the same count as the kept pass, with three fewer soft multipliers and one fewer soft adder, which should also free DSP capacity for the remaining multipliers of lines 335-343 (the kept pass packed 7 tiles against the baseline's 6).

No ASSUMES: the rewrite is unconditional — it holds for every value of the inputs, including the testbench's stimulus, because the folding is an identity mod 2^7 and 2^7 is exactly the output width.



## Simulation
When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of `bilinearintrp` on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.
response

I'll start by reading the relevant parts of the design.

Algebra and timing check out against the source. Now the edit.

The rewrite is in place and verified. bash check.sh raygentop.v passes with int_sop_2 3 in the census, and the reference testbench run locally (iverilog, seed 0x2545f491) reports result: pass — 0 mismatches on all three outputs over 1052 compared cycles, no X on the DUT.

What I changed, region by region

All nine regions are in module bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762). They collapse into three int_sop_2 instances, one per colour sum.

Regions 2874 (ul*rul), 2875 (vl*rvl), 2876 (wl*rwl) → blk_r. The two u/v products become the block's two product slots, with each colour coefficient replaced by its difference against the w coefficient: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dr} with dr = ru - rw, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,er} with er = rv - rw. The w product is folded in algebraically: since r is 7 bits wide, only the sum mod 2^7 is observable, wl = (255-u-v) mod 256 gives wl*rw ≡ -rw - u*rw - v*rw (mod 128), so r ≡ u*dr + v*er - rw. The leftover constant -rw stays outside as a 7-bit subtract. result goes to a new sum_r[36:0]; chainout is left unconnected (nothing reads it).

Regions 2877, 2878, 2879 → blk_g and 2880, 2881, 2882 → blk_b: identical, with dg = gu - gw, eg = gv - gw and db = bu - bw, eb = bv - bw.

Supporting changes.

  • Deleted the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1r…i3b, rul…bwl (old lines 2796–2816). Each was a local reg read only by the converted products; the module's only outputs are r, g, b. Kept u, v and left the selectuv mux always-block untouched — u/v now feed the blocks unregistered, the blocks' input registers taking the place of ul/vl and of rul…bwl.
  • Replaced the sequential always block with six 7-bit delay registers (rw_d1/rw_d2, gw_d1/gw_d2, bw_d1/bw_d2) that match the block's two register stages, so the folded term stays cycle-aligned.
  • Replaced lines 2884–2886 with assign r = (sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2); and likewise for g, b.

Registers and reset. Stage count is unchanged at 2: block input registers at edge k (was ul,vl,rul…), block result register at edge k+1 (was i1*,i2*,i3*), then one combinational 7-bit subtract (was the combinational 3-way add). bilinearintrp has no reset port and clears no register, so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks. The result registers and the six added delay registers are X at power-up and load every edge, so outputs are defined from the second clock edge, inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in; nothing relies on an added register's initial value.

Tie-offs. mode_sigs = 12'b0 and chainin = 37'b0 on every block; both product slots are used on every block, so no product-term port is tied off; every input port is connected by name. No chaining is used.

No ASSUMES — the folding is an exact identity mod 2^7, and 2^7 is exactly the output width, so it holds for every input value including the testbench's stimulus. Within the blocks nothing wraps: u,v ≤ 255 and dr,er ≤ 127, so the sum is at most 64770, far under 2^37, and the unsigned reading matches.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file raygentop.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three sums of module bilinearintrp — r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum, each now carrying all three products of that sum after the w term is folded into the two product slots
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • blk_r (sum of r): clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dr} with dr = ru - rw (7-bit wrap), bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,er} with er = rv - rw, chainin=37'b0, result→new wire sum_r[36:0], chainout unconnected.
  • blk_g: same, ay={12'b0,dg} with dg = gu - gw, by={12'b0,eg} with eg = gv - gw, result→sum_g[36:0].
  • blk_b: same, ay={12'b0,db} with db = bu - bw, by={12'b0,eb} with eb = bv - bw, result→sum_b[36:0].
  • u, v are the combinational 8-bit selectuv mux outputs (always block, lines 2818-2858), fed unregistered; the block's input registers replace ul, vl. ru,rv,rw,gu,gv,gw,bu,bv,bw are module inputs (driven at line 2012 from shadedata, which is tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input — lines 628-676); the 7-bit differences are combinational on them and the block's input registers replace rul, rvl, rwl, gul, ….
  • Outside the block, per colour, two added 7-bit delay registers (rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2) and a 7-bit subtract: assign r = sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2; and likewise for g, b.

PLAN:

Why this revision. The kept pass put six of the nine products into three blocks and left wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl as soft multipliers, because a third product per sum would need a chained second block and that costs a cycle. This pass removes those three soft multipliers without a fourth block and without a latency change, by folding the w term algebraically into the two product slots the block already has. The measured critical path of the kept pass ends at a block input register (mux → DSP input), and the block inputs here come from primary-input pads for the colour operands, so this change does not lengthen any timed path; it only deletes logic (3 soft 8×7 multipliers, the 255-u-v adder, and 74 flip-flops, against 6 small 7-bit subtracters and 42 flip-flops added), and it also drops two fanout branches (wl adder, i3* multipliers) off the critical mux outputs u/v.

The algebra (exact, not approximate). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits wide (lines 2786-2791), so only the sum mod 2^7 is observable. For colour r, with wl = (255-u-v) mod 256:

r ≡ u*ru + v*rv + wl*rw (mod 128)

The mod-256 wrap of wl contributes multiples of 256*rw ≡ 0 (mod 128), so wl*rw ≡ (255-u-v)*rw, and 255-u-v = 256-(u+v+1) ≡ -(u+v+1) (mod 128). Hence

r ≡ u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128)

Let dr = (ru-rw) mod 128 and er = (rv-rw) mod 128 (plain 7-bit two's-complement wrap-around subtraction of two 7-bit values). Since u*(dr-(ru-rw)) is u times a multiple of 128, u*dr ≡ u*(ru-rw) (mod 128), and likewise for v. So

r ≡ (u*dr + v*er) - rw (mod 128)

which is exactly result[6:0] - rw_delayed. The block reads its operands unsigned: u,v ≤ 255, dr,er ≤ 127, so each product ≤ 32385 and the sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^37, nothing wraps in the block, and the unsigned reading matches. Same for g and b.

Register stages. Original, per converted region: 2 stages from the operands (u/v from the mux, and the colour inputs) to the output — operand registers ul,vl,rul,rvl,rwl at edge k, product registers i1*,i2*,i3* at edge k+1, then a combinational add into the 7-bit output. After the rewrite: still 2 stages — the block's input registers at edge k, the block's result register at edge k+1, then one combinational 7-bit subtract. The folded w term keeps the same 2 stages through rw_d1 (edge k) and rw_d2 (edge k+1), so it is aligned with result and r/g/b change on exactly the same cycles as before. No chaining is used, so no block sees a 3-stage path.

The edit, concretely (module bilinearintrp, lines 2762-2887):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1r,i2r,i3r, i1g,i2g,i3g, i1b,i2b,i3b, rul,rvl,rwl, gul,gvl,gwl, bul,bvl,bwl (lines 2796-2816). Keep u, v (lines 2794-2795, the combinational mux outputs). Leave the mux always block (2818-2858) untouched.

  2. Add:

           wire[6:0] dr = ru - rw;  wire[6:0] er = rv - rw;
       wire[6:0] dg = gu - gw;  wire[6:0] eg = gv - gw;
       wire[6:0] db = bu - bw;  wire[6:0] eb = bv - bw;
       wire[36:0] sum_r, sum_g, sum_b;
       reg[6:0] rw_d1, rw_d2, gw_d1, gw_d2, bw_d1, bw_d2;

int_sop_2 blk_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dr}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,er}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_r), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_g (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,dg}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eg}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_g), .chainout());
int_sop_2 blk_b (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0,u}), .ay({12'b0,db}), .bx({10'b0,v}), .by({12'b0,eb}),
.chainin(37'b0), .result(sum_b), .chainout());

3. Replace the whole sequential always block (2860-2883) with:

always @(posedge clk)
begin
rw_d1 <= rw ; rw_d2 <= rw_d1 ;
gw_d1 <= gw ; gw_d2 <= gw_d1 ;
bw_d1 <= bw ; bw_d2 <= bw_d1 ;
end

4. Replace lines 2884-2886 with:

assign r = (sum_r[6:0] - rw_d2) ;
assign g = (sum_g[6:0] - gw_d2) ;
assign b = (sum_b[6:0] - bw_d2) ;


**Visibility.** Every signal removed (`ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl, i1*,i2*,i3*`) is a local `reg` of `bilinearintrp`; the module's only outputs are `r`, `g`, `b` (2786-2791). I checked the whole module body, 2762-2887: `ul`/`vl` are read only by the converted products, `wl` only by the three `i3*` products, the `*l` operand registers only by their own product. The `ul`/`vl` at lines 2752-2756 belong to the preceding module (which ends at 2761), not to this one. Nothing reads `chainout`; it is left unconnected on all three blocks.

**Reset and power-up.** `bilinearintrp` has no reset port and no register in it is ever cleared, so `reset` is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks; the testbench's reset policy is "none" (tb_equiv_bilin.v line 5), so there is no reset event for which behaviour could diverge. The three block result registers and the six added delay registers are X at power-up and all load every edge, so every output is defined from the second clock edge — well inside the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in, which is excluded from the comparison. Nothing relies on an added register's initial value.

**Tie-offs.** `mode_sigs` = 12'b0 and `chainin` = 37'b0 (constant) on all three blocks; both product slots are used on every block, so no product-term port is tied to 0; every input port is connected by name.

**Candidates converted and left.** All nine candidates are converted; no soft multiplier remains in `bilinearintrp`. The six `u`/`v` products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) go into the blocks' product slots directly, each with its colour coefficient replaced by the difference against the `w` coefficient; the three `w` products (2876, 2879, 2882) are folded into those same two slots by the identity above, with only their constant part (`-rw`, `-gw`, `-bw` mod 128) left as a 7-bit subtract outside — a term with no matching block port, computed outside the block as the rules allow. No candidate is left for "not chosen this pass" and none is left for a correctness reason.

**Blocks added:** 3 (3 of the device's 132 DSP tiles, each in sop_2 mode taking its tile alone) — the same count as the kept pass, with three fewer soft multipliers and one fewer soft adder, which should also free DSP capacity for the remaining multipliers of lines 335-343 (the kept pass packed 7 tiles against the baseline's 6).

No ASSUMES: the rewrite is unconditional — it holds for every value of the inputs, including the testbench's stimulus, because the folding is an identity mod 2^7 and 2^7 is exactly the output width.

DESIGN_CHANGE: `r`, `g`, `b` are 7 bits while the products are computed at 15 bits; the intermediate `i1*/i2*/i3*` registers and the final adders could be declared at 7 bits directly, which is all the design observes. Separately, if one extra cycle of latency on `blr/blg/blb` (instance at line 2012) were acceptable, the three sums could instead be chained block-to-block, but that is not needed now that all nine products fit in three blocks.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the int_sop_2 contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance. The edit touches only bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2794-2888); the harness's subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms the rest of the file is unchanged. All nine named regions (the products at original lines 2874-2882) are gone, replaced by the three blocks the plan specifies, with exactly the declarations, instantiations, delay registers and output assigns listed in steps 1-4. sum_r/g/b drive only r/g/b as planned; chainout is unconnected on all three.

Ports. Every input is connected by name on each block: clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u} (18), ay={12'b0,d*} (19), bx={10'b0,v} (18), by={12'b0,e*} (19), chainin=37'b0 (a constant, which the tie-off rule permits). Both product slots are used, so no product port is tied off. chainout() is read by nothing, satisfying the routing restriction.

Algebra. r is 7 bits (2787), so only mod-2^7 matters. wl = (8'hFF - u - v) mod 256, and 256 ≡ 0 (mod 128), so wl*rw ≡ (255-u-v)*rw ≡ -(u+v+1)*rw. With dr = (ru-rw) mod 128 (7-bit context on 2806, so the wrap is exactly mod 128) and likewise er, u*dr ≡ u*(ru-rw) and v*er ≡ v*(rv-rw) mod 128. Hence u*dr + v*er - rw ≡ u*ru + v*rv + wl*rw (mod 128) — an identity for all input values, so no ASSUMES is needed and none is given. Unsigned reading is correct: u,v ≤ 255, dr,er ≤ 127, products ≤ 32385, sum ≤ 64770, far below 2^37, so the block never wraps. On the original side the 15-bit i* registers also never truncate (32385 < 32768), and the final 15-bit add only matters in its low 7 bits.

Timing/registers. Original: operand registers at edge k, product registers at k+1, combinational add → 2 stages. New: block input registers at k, result at k+1, combinational 7-bit subtract → 2 stages. The folded -rw term is aligned through rw_d1/rw_d2 (same two edges), so r/g/b change on the same cycles; the impulse probe (sim_run.log:24-27) confirms latency offset 0 with the response at +2.

Reset/power-up. The module has no reset and no original register is ever cleared, so reset=1'b0 reproduces behaviour at every edge, not just start-up; testbench reset policy is "none". The added registers and the block result register are X until the second edge, within the excluded 8-cycle lead-in, and nothing relies on their initial value.

Visibility. Every removed signal (ul, vl, wl, i1*/i2*/i3*, rul…bwl) was a local reg of bilinearintrp; a file-wide grep finds no remaining reader — the ul/vl uses at 2706-2756 are the preceding module's own 7-bit regs declared at 2706-2707. The module's only outputs, r/g/b, keep their values and timing.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -1.44% against first, min-gain 0.046 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs)))
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 4.7578e+06 · critical path 4.84712 ns · wirelength 25664 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 2.30616e+07 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.60%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 4.60%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
